```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-no-cover-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r\\ge1$ be an integer, set $m_r=100r+10$ and $q_r=2^{-m_r}$, and let $s$ be a power of two with $s\\ge2^{m_r}$. Suppose $T_1,\\ldots,T_s$ are independent copies of a random tensor of total component rank at most $r$. If every fixed componentwise cover with dimension at most $rs$ in each mode has probability less than $p$, then $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^sT_j\\right)<q_rs/4\\right\\}\n \\le 4^s p^{q_rs/2}.\n \\label{eq:no-cover-rank}$$ Here rank means the sum of component ranks.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-injection",
        "exact_quote": "Let a prepared marked-unit law have final pins of total dimension at most $K$, and mixed marginals at most $M2^{o(N)}\\mu$. Let ${\\cal A}$ be a pair event for independent draws, of probability at least $N^{-c_0}$ for some fixed $c_0$. For each fixed draw suppose its accepting opposite units form a member of a fixed family of cardinality at most $2^{C_sN}$. Assume $C_s$ is fixed before $h$. For every fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, by choosing $h$ sufficiently large in terms of $K,C_s,\\varepsilon,g$, the proportion of pairs in ${\\cal A}$ failing the small-table injection conditions is at most $\\varepsilon$, for all sufficiently large $n$. For the unconditional pair event the accepting family has one member.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tiny-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the second alternative of [thm:phase-alternative](#thm:phase-alternative) holds, and write $$F(A,B)=u_{B,\\{1,2\\}}(\\Delta_A).$$ Then the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *The matrix $F$ on the finite support has rank at most $d_0N$. Its alternating part $F+F^{\\mathsf T}$ has rank at most $2d_0N$. If $\\mathbb P\\{F(A,B)+F(B,A)=0\\}\\to0$, there is a restriction of mass $2^{-o(N)}$ on which that alternating part is identically zero.*\n\n2.  *If $F(A,A)=0$ throughout, then for either fixed $c\\in\\mathbb F_2$, $$\\mathbb P\\{u_{B,1}(\\Delta_A)=u_{B,2}(\\Delta_A)\n           =u_{A,1}(\\Delta_B)=u_{A,2}(\\Delta_B)=c\\}\n           \\ge c_1N^{-4}\n     \\label{eq:tiny-four-phases}$$ for a positive fixed $c_1$ and all large $n$.*\n\n3.  *In part <span class=\"upright\">(b)</span>, one can apply the second peeling so that the event in [eq:tiny-four-phases](#eq:tiny-four-phases) is determined by the two leaves. At most $4K_1+2d_0<u_0$ additional image directions are needed. The same $\\Omega(N^{-4})$ leaf-pair mass survives the exponentially small discards. On admissible small tables, the contractions on the old $\\lambda_i$’s then equal the actual contractions.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tiny-cover",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose the second alternative of [thm:phase-alternative](#thm:phase-alternative) holds, and write $$F(A,B)=u_{B,\\{1,2\\}}(\\Delta_A).$$ Then the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *The matrix $F$ on the finite support has rank at most $d_0N$. Its alternating part $F+F^{\\mathsf T}$ has rank at most $2d_0N$. If $\\mathbb P\\{F(A,B)+F(B,A)=0\\}\\to0$, there is a restriction of mass $2^{-o(N)}$ on which that alternating part is identically zero.*\n\n2.  *If $F(A,A)=0$ throughout, then for either fixed $c\\in\\mathbb F_2$, $$\\mathbb P\\{u_{B,1}(\\Delta_A)=u_{B,2}(\\Delta_A)\n           =u_{A,1}(\\Delta_B)=u_{A,2}(\\Delta_B)=c\\}\n           \\ge c_1N^{-4}\n     \\label{eq:tiny-four-phases}$$ for a positive fixed $c_1$ and all large $n$.*\n\n3.  *In part <span class=\"upright\">(b)</span>, one can apply the second peeling so that the event in [eq:tiny-four-phases](#eq:tiny-four-phases) is determined by the two leaves.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-status-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "Along a subsequence, the unit law can be prepared with final pin dimension at most $K$ and the final per-leaf bounds of [lem:peeling](#lem:peeling), at relative mass cost $2^{-o(N)}$, so that one of the following descriptions applies.*\n\n1.  *There is no prediction as in [def:prediction](#def:prediction) on the first-peeled law. The effective spaces are still the first ones. For independent units, the mass admitting an admissible injecting small table is greater than $.99$.*\n\n2.  *A prediction with fixed $\\alpha_i,c$ and common classes $e_i$ is retained. The old marks and exceptions are preserved, and [eq:prediction](#eq:prediction) has uniform $o(1)$ error on every retained unit. The mass admitting an admissible injecting table satisfying [eq:phase-compatibility](#eq:phase-compatibility) is bounded below by a positive constant.*\n\n3.  *Both prediction classes are zero, and every retained unit satisfies the exact identities $$p_i=T(\\lambda_i,\\cdot)+a\n     \\quad\\text{on all of }\\mathcal X,\\qquad i=1,2.$$ There is a set of leaf pairs of mass at least $c_2N^{-4}$ under the final leaf mixture, for a fixed $c_2>0$, with phase compatibility determined by the two leaves. For each such pair one can choose a specific injecting compatible table whose two separate orientation admissibility filters have masses at least $a_0>0$. The constants $c_2,a_0$ are independent of $n$.*\n\n*In every case the mixed joint density is at most $2^{(D+o(1))N}\\mu^2$, and its individual marginals are at most $M2^{o(N)}\\mu$. Relative to the stored leaf bases and pin images, all numerical table formats, unary statuses, old-mark coordinates and exception-set descriptions have bounded length in $n$. The extra exact pins are used only in case <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span>.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-status-preparation",
          "exact_quote": "Along a subsequence, the unit law can be prepared with final pin dimension at most $K$ and the final per-leaf bounds of [lem:peeling](#lem:peeling), at relative mass cost $2^{-o(N)}$, so that one of the following descriptions applies.*\n\n1.  *There is no prediction as in [def:prediction](#def:prediction) on the first-peeled law. The effective spaces are still the first ones. For each such pair one can choose a specific injecting compatible table whose two separate orientation admissibility filters have masses at least $a_0>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-slice",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a selector, a flavor, and a fixed number $d$ of affine parameter variables. Write a random affine coefficient map as $$C(t,u)=p_s\\otimes(t,z_0t+Lu),\n \\qquad (t,u)\\in\\mathbb F_2\\oplus\\mathbb F_2^d.$$ Assume the flavor retains an $n$-bit $Z$-block absent from $E$. Suppose we condition the affine coefficients on $C^{\\mathsf T}EC=G$, where $G$ is fixed, this event has probability at least a fixed $\\beta>0$, and it depends on no $Z$ coefficient. Let ${\\cal T}_n$ be any common test of absolute value at most one on the image column arrays $(P_eC,Q_eC)_e$, using a fixed set of components. Its empirical average over the conditioned affine coefficients, at a raw orientation, has variance $$O(2^{-N/2}+2^{2d+1-n})$$ around its mean under uniform injective image column arrays with internal Gram $G$. The constants may depend on the fixed column counts and $\\beta$, but the estimate is uniform over the tests ${\\cal T}_n$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-histogram-control",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a query shape, a constant $C_L$, and an integer $F$. Consider any collection of normalized leaf laws $\\pi$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:histogram-leaf-cap}\n \\pi\\le 2^{C_LN}\\mu^2.$$ The following assertions are uniform over those leaves and over the bounded numerical choices of a query.*\n\n1.  *If $A_n$ is a sequence of slot sets with $\\omega_n(A_n)\\to0$, then the probability that the unfiltered query key belongs to $A_n$, averaged on any choice of leaves, tends to zero. Equivalently, the averaged key laws are uniformly absolutely continuous in this asymptotic small-set sense.*\n\n2.  *On each leaf, choose any $F$ binary flags of the orientation and query parameters. Let $\\mathcal H_{\\pi,n}$ be the family of unnormalized key densities obtained by filtering by any event of the orientation and the flag values. For every $\\varepsilon>0$, this family has an $L^1(\\omega_n)$ $\\varepsilon$-net of cardinality bounded independently of $n$ and of the leaf, for all sufficiently large $n$. The net itself may depend on the leaf.*\n\n3.  *Both conclusions persist after restricting the query to $\\mathcal K_n$ and expressing the subdensities relative to $\\nu_n$. In particular these subdensities are uniformly integrable: for every $\\eta>0$ there is $R<\\infty$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, all their tails above $R$ have integral at most $\\eta$.*\n\n*The flag functions may vary with the leaf and with $n$. The number of flags is fixed. No bound on the normalized marginal of an individual leaf is assumed here.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-histogram-gram-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $a$ plus slots and $b$ minus slots in one ambient component be independent uniform vectors, and prescribe their entire $a$ by $b$ Gram matrix. If $a+b\\le N/2$, the probability of that prescription together with injectivity in each mode is $$2^{-ab}\\bigl(1+O(2^{-N+a+b})\\bigr),$$ uniformly in the prescribed matrix. The same statement holds for a fixed collection of independent endpoint/component groups, with the sum of their Gram-bit counts in place of $ab$ and a fixed factor in the error bound. In particular, for a bounded number of slots all relevant Gram normalizations and their ratios are bounded above and below by positive constants, uniformly in the nominal inputs.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-histogram-batch-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two bounded batches of prescribed nominal columns in one raw endpoint, or in two independent raw endpoints. Suppose their union is nominally independent in every endpoint, component, and mode. Let $F$ and $G$ be bounded functions of the separate image batches, respectively. The difference between their joint raw expectation and the product of their separate raw expectations is $$O\\bigl(\\lVert F\\rVert_\\infty\\lVert G\\rVert_\\infty 2^{-N/2}\\bigr).$$ The bound is uniform in the independent nominal columns and all their prescribed same-endpoint Gram entries. The functions may also depend on the fixed nominal inputs determining their own batch.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-histogram-many-queries",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a bounded query shape. There are constants $c_1,c_2>0$, independent of any key-space partition, with the following property. Put $\\ell=\\lfloor c_1n\\rfloor$ and repeat the query independently $\\ell$ times at a common orientation. Let $A_1,\\ldots,A_\\ell$ be cells of a partition of the full slot space, with weights $w_j=\\omega_n(A_j)$. Suppose the partition has bounded size and its nonempty weights have a positive lower bound along the sequence under consideration. Conditional on any nominal inputs whose queried columns are jointly independent in every endpoint, component, and mode, the raw image probability that query $j$ lies in $A_j$ is at most $$\\label{eq:histogram-many-query-bound}\n 2^{c_2\\ell}\\prod_{j=1}^{\\ell}w_j$$ for all sufficiently large $n$. How large $n$ must be may depend on the partition and its weight bound. The constants $c_1,c_2$ do not.*\n\n*The probability, over the repeated fresh parameters, that the required nominal independence fails tends to zero exponentially in $n$, uniformly over the common orientation.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-histogram-many-queries",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a bounded query shape. There are constants $c_1,c_2>0$, independent of any key-space partition, with the following property. Let $A_1,\\ldots,A_\\ell$ be cells of a partition of the full slot space, with weights $w_j=\\omega_n(A_j)$. Suppose the partition has bounded size and its nonempty weights have a positive lower bound along the sequence under consideration. Conditional on any nominal inputs whose queried columns are jointly independent in every endpoint, component, and mode, the raw image probability that query $j$ lies in $A_j$ is at most $$\\label{eq:histogram-many-query-bound}\n 2^{c_2\\ell}\\prod_{j=1}^{\\ell}w_j$$ for all sufficiently large $n$. How large $n$ must be may depend on the partition and its weight bound. The constants $c_1,c_2$ do not.*\n\n*The probability, over the repeated fresh parameters, that the required nominal independence fails tends to zero exponentially in $n$, uniformly over the common orientation.",
          "source_id": "PM-157.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-typicality",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_n$ be a uniformly bounded common function of the inputs and keys of a fixed bounded query, with no other orientation dependence. Put $$A_n(O)=\\mathbb E_z F_n(z,K(O,z)),\\qquad\n m_n=\\mathbb E_{O\\sim\\mu^2,z}F_n(z,K(O,z)).$$ For every fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, under <a href=\"#eq:histogram-mixed-caps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:histogram-mixed-caps\">[eq:histogram-mixed-caps]</a>, $$\\rho_n\\{\\lvert A_n-m_n\\rvert>\\varepsilon\\}=2^{-\\Omega(n)}.$$ Repeated labels in the query are allowed. The constants may depend on the query and $\\varepsilon$, but the estimate is uniform over tests with the given sup bound. The exceptional set may depend on the test.*\n\n*For a query at just one endpoint, its raw variance about its raw mean is $2^{-\\Omega(n)}$. This variance bound is also uniform when an arbitrary external input-and-image datum is fixed as an argument of the test.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-typicality",

The constants may depend on the query and $\\varepsilon$, but the estimate is uniform over tests with the given sup bound.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-binary-mixing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a query template whose labels are distinct at each used endpoint. Impose the off-position Gram zeros of $\\mathcal K_n$ and a consistent fixed specification of the within-endpoint ordered $L,R$ evaluations used in [sec:realization](#sec:realization). Let $\\mathcal A_n(O,z)$ be their joint indicator. There is a template constant $\\kappa>0$ such that, outside an exponentially small set of orientations under <a href=\"#eq:histogram-mixed-caps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:histogram-mixed-caps\">[eq:histogram-mixed-caps]</a>, $$\\label{eq:binary-mixing}\n \\mathbb E_z\\mathcal A_n(O,z)\\prod_jw_j(O,z_j)\n =\\kappa\\prod_j\\mathbb E_{z_j}w_j(O,z_j)+O(2^{-c n})$$ uniformly over all unary weights with $\\lvert w_j\\rvert\\le1$. These weights may depend on the entire fixed orientation. The input laws may be conditioned separately on feasible diagonal values or tester summaries. The exceptional set is independent of the unary weights.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a quadratic function on a binary vector space and let its polar form have rank $2r$. Its uniform sign bias has magnitude at most $2^{-r}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-unary-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an endpoint, tag, label, flavor, and feasible tester summary with diagonal $q$. For a bounded number of common global key tests, its empirical conditional key frequencies differ from $\\Omega_{l,q,n}$ by any prescribed fixed tolerance except on exponentially small mixed mass under <a href=\"#eq:histogram-mixed-caps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:histogram-mixed-caps\">[eq:histogram-mixed-caps]</a>.*\n\n*At each unit, let $C_i\\subset\\mathcal X$ be any orientation-dependent space of dimension at most $K^2$ all of whose elements have total component rank at most $K$, and choose any basis. Fix a sequence of global single-key sets $A_n$ with $\\Omega_{l,q,n}(A_n)\\ge\\delta>0$. Except on exponentially small mixed mass, every basis assignment has, conditional on the tester summary and on $K_i\\in A_n$, fraction at least $(7/8)2^{-\\dim C_i}$.*\n\n\n\n*For the full generic flavor and inputs conditional just on $q$, the following conservative constant is valid: $$\\label{eq:uniform-base-fraction}\n \\beta=2^{-K^2-1}4^{-(g^2+1)}>0.$$ For every feasible specified tester summary and basis assignment, outside an exponentially small exceptional set, $$\\label{eq:histogram-base-cell-lower}\n \\mathbb P_z\\{K_i\\in A_n,\\ \\text{the specified summary and basis bits}\\mid q\\}\n \\ge\\beta\\,\\Omega_{l,q,n}(A_n).$$ In particular each joint choice of the role $a(\\operatorname{atom})$ and the basis bits has this lower bound, after choosing a compatible summary. Both roles are available for either possible generic $q$.*\n\n*The lower constants do not depend on $\\delta$, on the key tests, or on the orientation-dependent choice of $C_i$. The onset of the asymptotics and the exceptional-set constants may depend on a fixed positive lower bound $\\delta$. The exceptional set is allowed to depend on the prescribed tests: there is no simultaneous assertion over all measurable key sets at one orientation.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },

        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-weak-product",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W_n$ be any sequence of real functions on $\\mathcal K_n$ with $\\lVert W_n\\rVert_\\infty\\le B$, where $B$ is fixed. Extend it to the product of the single-key spaces with the same bound. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there are constants $L,C<\\infty$, independent of $n$, and functions $$\\label{eq:weak-product-approximant}\n S_n(k_1,\\ldots,k_r)\n   =\\sum_{a=1}^{L}c_{a,n}\\prod_{j=1}^{r}f_{a,j,n}(k_j),\n \\qquad\n \\lvert c_{a,n}\\rvert\\le C,\\quad \\lvert f_{a,j,n}\\rvert\\le1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *For arbitrary global unary functions $g_{j,n}$ of bound one, $$\\label{eq:weak-product-reference}\n     \\lvert \\int(W_n-S_n)\\prod_jg_{j,n}(k_j)\\,d\\nu_n\\rvert\\le\\varepsilon$$ for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n\n2.  *Under any mixed unit law satisfying <a href=\"#eq:histogram-mixed-caps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:histogram-mixed-caps\">[eq:histogram-mixed-caps]</a>, outside an exponentially small exceptional set of orientations, $$\\label{eq:weak-product-actual}\n     \\sup_{\\lvert w_j\\rvert\\le1}\n     \\lvert \\mathbb E_z\\mathcal A_n(O,z)\n           (W_n-S_n)(K(O,z))\\prod_jw_j(O,z_j)\\rvert\\le\\varepsilon.$$ The unary input weights may depend on the entire fixed orientation and on any additional marks fixed with it. The exceptional set is independent of these weights.*\n\n*The functions $f_{a,j,n}$ are global key functions; they can be chosen to be indicators of sets, or finite linear combinations of such indicators with uniformly bounded complexity. They do not depend on the sampled orientation. The constants may depend on $B,\\varepsilon$ and the fixed template and construction parameters, but not on $n$.*\n\n*An additional orientation-only filter of bound one may multiply <a href=\"#eq:weak-product-actual\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weak-product-actual\">[eq:weak-product-actual]</a> without changing it. In particular, after averaging normalized leaves, the same error control applies to unnormalized admissibility-filtered query measures, even when the filter is chosen using the opposite leaf. No assertion is made for an arbitrary additional joint input filter.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-weak-product",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W_n$ be any sequence of real functions on $\\mathcal K_n$ with $\\lVert W_n\\rVert_\\infty\\le B$, where $B$ is fixed. Extend it to the product of the single-key spaces with the same bound. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there are constants $L,C<\\infty$, independent of $n$, and functions $$\\label{eq:weak-product-approximant}\n S_n(k_1,\\ldots,k_r)\n   =\\sum_{a=1}^{L}c_{a,n}\\prod_{j=1}^{r}f_{a,j,n}(k_j),\n \\qquad\n \\lvert c_{a,n}\\rvert\\le C,\\quad \\lvert f_{a,j,n}\\rvert\\le1,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *For arbitrary global unary functions $g_{j,n}$ of bound one, $$\\label{eq:weak-product-reference}\n     \\lvert \\int(W_n-S_n)\\prod_jg_{j,n}(k_j)\\,d\\nu_n\\rvert\\le\\varepsilon$$ for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n\n2.  *Under any mixed unit law satisfying <a href=\"#eq:histogram-mixed-caps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:histogram-mixed-caps\">[eq:histogram-mixed-caps]</a>, outside an exponentially small exceptional set of orientations, $$\\label{eq:weak-product-actual}\n     \\sup_{\\lvert w_j\\rvert\\le1}\n     \\lvert \\mathbb E_z\\mathcal A_n(O,z)\n           (W_n-S_n)(K(O,z))\\prod_jw_j(O,z_j)\\rvert\\le\\varepsilon.$$ The unary input weights may depend on the entire fixed orientation and on any additional marks fixed with it. The exceptional set is independent of these weights.*\n\n*The functions $f_{a,j,n}$ are global key functions; they can be chosen to be indicators of sets, or finite linear combinations of such indicators with uniformly bounded complexity. They do not depend on the sampled orientation. The constants may depend on $B,\\varepsilon$ and the fixed template and construction parameters, but not on $n$.*\n\n*An additional orientation-only filter of bound one may multiply <a href=\"#eq:weak-product-actual\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weak-product-actual\">[eq:weak-product-actual]</a> without changing it.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-product-twisted-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\lvert W\\rvert\\le B$ on the product key space of $I\\cup J$, and let $\\delta>0$. There is a decomposition $$\\label{eq:twisted-regularity-decomposition}\n W=\\sum_{a=1}^{L'}c_a f_a(k_I)g_a(k_J)\\tau_a(k_I,k_J)+D,$$ where $\\lvert f_a\\rvert,\\lvert g_a\\rvert\\le1$, $\\tau_a\\in\\mathcal T_{I,J}$, $\\lvert c_a\\rvert\\le B$, $\\lVert D\\rVert_\\infty\\le2B$, and $\\lVert D\\rVert_{\\square,\\mathcal T}\\le\\delta$. The number $L'$ is bounded in terms of $B,\\delta$ and the twist count only. The functions $f_a,g_a$ may be chosen as indicators of cells in finite group partitions.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

The number $L'$ is bounded in terms of $B,\\delta$ and the twist count only. The functions $f_a,g_a$ may be chosen as indicators of cells in finite group partitions.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-product-terminal-remainder",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I,J$ be a nontrivial partition of some of the template positions. Let $D_n$ be a common function of their global keys with $\\lVert D_n\\rVert_\\infty\\le B_1$ and $\\lVert D_n\\rVert_{\\square,\\mathcal T}\\le\\delta$. Let $\\psi_n(I,J)$ be any product of the specified nominal $L,R$ signs and global Gram signs crossing from $I$ to $J$, evaluated on their actual inputs and keys. For each fixed $\\rho>0$, except on exponentially small mixed mass, $$\\label{eq:product-remainder-actual}\n \\sup_{\\lvert f\\rvert,\\lvert g\\rvert\\le1}\n \\lvert \\mathbb E_{z_I,z_J}D_n(K_I,K_J)\\psi_n(I,J)f(O,z_I)g(O,z_J)\\rvert\n \\le\\bigl(C_3\\delta+\\rho+o(1)\\bigr)^{1/4}.$$ The constant $C_3$ and the vanishing error depend only on $B_1$ and the fixed template. The weights may depend arbitrarily on the fixed orientation. The exceptional set does not depend on those weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-limit-global-projection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(K_n,\\nu_n)$ be finite probability spaces. On each first-side leaf $L$ and each second-side leaf $L'$ let there be a family of subdensities. Assume that, for each fixed $R<\\infty$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, their truncations at height $R$ have $L^1(\\nu_n)$ nets of uniformly bounded size, with the nets chosen using their own leaf only. The bounds may depend on $R,\\varepsilon$ but not on $n$ or the leaf.*\n\n\n*For every $R<\\infty$ and $\\eta>0$, there are deterministic partitions $\\mathcal P_n$ of uniformly bounded size such that the following holds. These partitions may depend on the leaf laws, but are chosen before the leaves are sampled. For independent leaves, choose a member $f$ and a member $g$ of the two truncated families, allowing either choice to depend on both leaves. If $P_n$ is conditional expectation onto $\\mathcal P_n$, then $$\\label{eq:limit-projection-error}\n\\mathbb E\\bigl|\\langle f,g\\rangle-\\langle P_nf,P_ng\\rangle\\bigr|\\le\\eta.$$ The same conclusion holds for every deterministic refinement of $\\mathcal P_n$ chosen independently of the sampled leaves. Multiplication of the discrepancy by any indicator determined by the leaf pair does not increase the bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-limit-global-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(K_n,\\nu_n)$ be finite probability spaces. On each first-side leaf $L$ and each second-side leaf $L'$ let there be a family of subdensities. Assume that, for each fixed $R<\\infty$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, their truncations at height $R$ have $L^1(\\nu_n)$ nets of uniformly bounded size, with the nets chosen using their own leaf only. The bounds may depend on $R,\\varepsilon$ but not on $n$ or the leaf.*\n\n\n*For every $R<\\infty$ and $\\eta>0$, there are deterministic partitions $\\mathcal P_n$ of uniformly bounded size such that the following holds. These partitions may depend on the leaf laws, but are chosen before the leaves are sampled. For independent leaves, choose a member $f$ and a member $g$ of the two truncated families, allowing either choice to depend on both leaves. If $P_n$ is conditional expectation onto $\\mathcal P_n$, then $$\\label{eq:limit-projection-error}\n\\mathbb E\\bigl|\\langle f,g\\rangle-\\langle P_nf,P_ng\\rangle\\bigr|\\le\\eta.$$ The same conclusion holds for every deterministic refinement of $\\mathcal P_n$ chosen independently of the sampled leaves. Multiplication of the discrepancy by any indicator determined by the leaf pair does not increase the bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-157.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(K_n,\\nu_n)$ be finite probability spaces. On each first-side leaf $L$ and each second-side leaf $L'$ let there be a family of subdensities. The bounds may depend on $R,\\varepsilon$ but not on $n$ or the leaf.*\n\n\n*For every $R<\\infty$ and $\\eta>0$, there are deterministic partitions $\\mathcal P_n$ of uniformly bounded size such that the following holds. These partitions may depend on the leaf laws, but are chosen before the leaves are sampled. For independent leaves, choose a member $f$ and a member $g$ of the two truncated families, allowing either choice to depend on both leaves. If $P_n$ is conditional expectation onto $\\mathcal P_n$, then $$\\label{eq:limit-projection-error}\n\\mathbb E\\bigl|\\langle f,g\\rangle-\\langle P_nf,P_ng\\rangle\\bigr|\\le\\eta.$$ The same conclusion holds for every deterministic refinement of $\\mathcal P_n$ chosen independently of the sampled leaves. Multiplication of the discrepancy by any indicator determined by the leaf pair does not increase the bound.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-limit-model",
        "exact_quote": "There is a subsequence and a common limiting experiment with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For each tag and diagonal bit $(l,q)$ there is a compact bit-sequence space $\\Xi_{l,q}$ with reference law $m_{l,q}$. For each key shape there is a compact tuple-test space $\\Upsilon$ with reference law $\\rho$. Its single-position projections $\\xi_\\ell$ have the independent laws $m_{l_\\ell,q_\\ell}$.*\n\n\n2.  *A base orientation array $\\theta$ contains its bounded discrete metadata and its single-atom status measures. For a unary type $t=(i,l,s,\\varphi,q)$ and a full numerical status $\\upsilon$, these measures have jointly measurable densities $$\\label{eq:limit-unary-densities}\n    h^\\theta_{t,\\upsilon}:\\Xi_{l,q}\\longrightarrow[0,1],\\qquad\n    \\sum_\\upsilon h^\\theta_{t,\\upsilon}=1\\quad m_{l,q}\\text{-almost everywhere}.$$ For the full generic flavor, the sum of these densities over statuses with any specified role and basis vector in the recorded actual dimension is bounded below by a positive construction constant, almost everywhere. Compatible finer tester requirements have the lower fractions supplied by [lem:unary-positivity](#lem:unary-positivity).*\n\n3.  *There is first a random limiting leaf-pair record $Z$, which includes two conditional laws $\\Lambda_A,\\Lambda_B$ of the arrays and their finite flag vectors. Conditional on $Z$, the two orientation records are drawn independently from these laws. After flags are dropped and $Z$ is averaged out, $\\theta_A,\\theta_B$ are iid.*\n\n4.  *Every discrete assertion of table usability, injection, and prepared phase compatibility is retained in this experiment. In particular, the positive mass of a passed compatible injecting table from [prop:status-preparation](#prop:status-preparation) is retained. On the no-prediction branch the mass of a passed injecting table is at least $.99$. On a predicted branch the prediction relation is exact in the limiting status measures outside its stored exceptions.*\n\n*All single-key tests used to define $\\Xi_{l,q}$ are functions of global keys only.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-limit-model",

For a unary type $t=(i,l,s,\\varphi,q)$ and a full numerical status $\\upsilon$, these measures have jointly measurable densities $$\\label{eq:limit-unary-densities}\n    h^\\theta_{t,\\upsilon}:\\Xi_{l,q}\\longrightarrow[0,1],\\qquad\n    \\sum_\\upsilon h^\\theta_{t,\\upsilon}=1\\quad m_{l,q}\\text{-almost everywhere}.$$ For the full generic flavor, the sum of these densities over statuses with any specified role and basis vector in the recorded actual dimension is bounded below by a positive construction constant, almost everywhere. Compatible finer tester requirements have the lower fractions supplied by [lem:unary-positivity](#lem:unary-positivity).*\n\n3.  *There is first a random limiting leaf-pair record $Z$, which includes two conditional laws $\\Lambda_A,\\Lambda_B$ of the arrays and their finite flag vectors. Conditional on $Z$, the two orientation records are drawn independently from these laws. After flags are dropped and $Z$ is averaged out, $\\theta_A,\\theta_B$ are iid.*\n\n4.  *Every discrete assertion of table usability, injection, and prepared phase compatibility is retained in this experiment. In particular, the positive mass of a passed compatible injecting table from [prop:status-preparation](#prop:status-preparation) is retained. On the no-prediction branch the mass of a passed injecting table is at least $.99$. On a predicted branch the prediction relation is exact in the limiting status measures outside its stored exceptions.*\n\n*All single-key tests used to define $\\Xi_{l,q}$ are functions of global keys only.",

        "exact_quote": "Any obstruction to the scalar system has a nonzero vector of weights $(s_1,s_2,r_1,r_2)\\in\\mathbb F_2^4$. On every edge span $V_{ij}$ these give a relation $$\\label{eq:status-dual-relation}\ns_j(p_{A,i}+h_{A,i})+r_i(p_{B,j}+h_{B,j})\n=\\mathbf c_{ij}\\cdot\\mathbf x_{A,i}\n +\\mathbf c'_{ij}\\cdot\\mathbf x_{B,j}\n +\\beta_{ij}(h_{A,i}+h_{B,j})+\\gamma_{ij}q$$ for suitable coefficients. The scalar system is soluble if, for every such relation, the sum of the right sides evaluated at the local targets is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-157.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **connected matching number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *connected matching* is a matching whose edges are pairwise touching; write $\mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)$ for its maximum size.
- **Hadwiger number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Thus $h(G)$ is the largest $t$ for which $G$ contains a $K_t$ minor.
- **fractional chromatic number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The ordinary fractional chromatic number $\chi_f(G)$ is the minimum of $\sum_Iw_I$ over these assignments.
- **hole** ([source](#stmt-def-hole)): Two elements $i,j\in\Omega$ have a *hole* between them if there exist $\lambda_i,\lambda_j\in\mathcal X$ such that $$\label{eq:hole-equations} \begin{gathered} U_i\lambda_i=U_j\lambda_j,\qquad u_jU_i=r(\lambda_i),\qquad u_iU_j=r(\lambda_j),\\ a(\lambda_i)+a(\lambda_j)=1. \end{gathered}$$ The middle two identities are identities of linear functionals on the whole space $\mathcal X$.
- **atom and flavor** ([source](#stmt-def-atom)): For a tag $l$, selector $s$, and base point $z$ allowed at $l$, let $\mathfrak a_l(s,z)\in\mathcal X$ be the cut profile represented by $w_l=v(s,z)v(s,z)^{\mathsf T}$ and $w_t=0$ for $t\ne l$. Thus its component is $v(s,z)v(s,z)^{\mathsf T}$ at every component incident with $l$, and zero elsewhere. Its diagonal bit is $q=\eta(w_l)$ and its role bit is $a(\mathfrak a_l(s,z))$. A generic flavor retains all base coordinates allowed at $l$. A shared-only flavor sets all $O$ blocks to zero. A pure flavor sets $S$ to zero and retains a specified subset of the allowed $O$ blocks, setting the others to zero. Every flavor retains both $\#$ and $Z$ in their entirety. At a fixed label, a parameter test samples all retained bits independently and uniformly. Its tester summary is the list of all individual quadratic tester values. In addition to this summary we may record the basis values of $T(\mathfrak a_l(s,z),\cdot)\|_{C_i}$.
- **small table** ([source](#stmt-def-small-table)): For a leaf and component, put $$\mathcal J_{O,e}^{\pm} =\bigoplus_{i\in O}(S_{i,e}^{\pm}\oplus H_i^{\pm}).$$ For two leaves belonging to units $A,B$, a small table specifies the two cross bilinear forms on $\mathcal J_{A,e}^+\times\mathcal J_{B,e}^-$ and $\mathcal J_{B,e}^+\times\mathcal J_{A,e}^-$, for every $e$. It is admissible for two orientations if it agrees with their actual cross Grams whenever at least one of the two table arguments is a stored pin direction. Let $H_{i,0}^{\pm}$ be the projection of the appropriate pin space onto $H_i^{\pm}$, and fix a complement $H_i^{\pm}=H_{i,0}^{\pm}\oplus H_{i,1}^{\pm}$. The complement is called private. We identify the two channel coordinate copies with $\mathbb F_2^h$ in their common column order. The table is injecting if, for each component, each orientation of the roles $O,O'$, and $i\in O,z\in O'$, the following maps are injective: $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:table-injection} D_{O,e}^+\cap\mathcal B_i&\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2^h/H_{z,0}^+, &d&\longmapsto\text{table evaluation of $d$ against $Y_z$},\\ D_{O,e}^-\cap\mathcal B_i&\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2^h/H_{z,0}^-, &d&\longmapsto\text{table evaluation of $d$ against $X_z$}. \notag \end{aligned}$$ These quotient conditions use the standard coordinate dot pairing on the channels. For instance, the first image is zero in the quotient precisely when it pairs to zero with all vectors annihilating $H_{z,0}^+$.
- **componentwise cover** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A componentwise cover is a collection of subspaces $T_e^\pm\subseteq V_e^\pm$. It covers a tensor $\xi=(\xi_e)_e$ if $$\xi_e\in T_e^+\otimes V_e^-+V_e^+\otimes T_e^- \quad\text{for every }e.$$ Its two mode dimensions are $\sum_e\dim T_e^+$ and $\sum_e\dim T_e^-$; its total dimension is their sum.
- **common prediction** ([source](#stmt-def-prediction)): A prediction along a subsequence consists of common binary functions $$f_{i,n}(l,q,\cdot):\Omega_{l,q,n}\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2, \qquad i=1,2,$$ a set of units of mass at least $10^{-6}$, and, on every unit in this set, choices $$\lambda_i\in C_i^{\mathrm{old}},\qquad \alpha_i\in\mathbb F_2,\qquad {\cal E}_i\subseteq\mathbb F_2^b,\quad \|{\cal E}_i\|\le B_*,$$ such that the following uniform statement holds. There is $\varepsilon_n\to0$ for which, at both endpoints, at every label outside ${\cal E}_i$, at every tag, and in every permitted flavor, a uniform parameter atom $x$ satisfies $$p_i(x)=T(\lambda_i,x)+\alpha_i a(x) +f_{i,n}(l,q,\operatorname{key}(x)) \label{eq:prediction}$$ except with probability at most $\varepsilon_n$. The functions $f_{i,n}$ are the same for all retained units. The marks $\lambda_i,\alpha_i,{\cal E}_i$ may depend on the whole unit.
- **numerical option** ([source](#stmt-def-limit-option)): A numerical option specifies a recipe shape with between one and seven positions on each cross edge, together with the following data: 1. the endpoint, tag, label, flavor, and diagonal bit at every position; 2. the full numerical unary statuses required at those positions, including the tester summary, the role $a(\mathrm{atom})$, the basis coordinates of $T(\mathrm{atom},\cdot)\|_{C_i}$, and the $p_i$ bit; 3. the required within-endpoint binary evaluations, and the off-position Gram conditions defining the reference key space; 4. the small tables and the finite coordinate formats in which they are written. The two sides have matching tags and diagonal bits position by position. All labels used at one endpoint are distinct. Coordinate formats of smaller dimension are padded by zeros.
- **available generator** ([source](#stmt-def-status-generator)): On the cross edge $(i,j)$, an available generator consists of a vector $$\label{eq:status-generator-vector} g=(q,h_{A,i},h_{B,j},\mathbf x_{A,i},\mathbf x_{B,j},p_{A,i},p_{B,j})$$ and a *certificate*: a common tag, a surviving label at each end, and allowed flavors giving positive overlap as in <a href="#eq:limit-positive-generator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-positive-generator">[eq:limit-positive-generator]</a> for these entries. Entries of the tester summary not displayed in <a href="#eq:status-generator-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:status-generator-vector">[eq:status-generator-vector]</a> are summed over. All witnessing certificates remain available. Generators with the same numerical vector and different certificates may therefore be used as different occurrences. Sums and spans of generators refer to their numerical vectors. Let $V_{ij}$ be the binary span of these generators. Its *base projection* forgets the final two $p$ entries and has target $$B_{ij}=\mathbb F_2\oplus\mathbb F_2\oplus\mathbb F_2\oplus \mathbb F_2^{\dim C_{A,i}}\oplus\mathbb F_2^{\dim C_{B,j}}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-cm-bound | connected matching number of the constructed graph | < | $\frac{m}{100}$ | arbitrarily large m |
| q-independence | independence number | ≤ | $2$ | arbitrarily large m |
| q-hadwiger-upper | Hadwiger number | < | $\frac{26m}{75}+\frac23$ | order m |
| q-fractional-lower | fractional chromatic number | ≥ | $\frac m2$ | order m |
| q-minor-matching-bound | Hadwiger number in terms of connected matching number | ≤ | $\frac{m+4\mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)+2}{3}$ | all m |
| q-four-hole-probability | probability of all four cross holes | ≥ | $2^{-100gN}$ | sufficiently large n |
| q-gram-probability-upper | probability of prescribed Gram matrix with injective frames | ≤ | $2^{-pq}$ | p+q at most N |
| q-walsh-correlation | bilinear character correlation | ≤ | $2^{-d/2}$ | bilinear form of rank d |
| q-rare-overlap-order | expected overlap on compatible leaf pairs | ≥ | $\Omega(N^{-4})$ | large n |
| q-constant-C0 | early constant C_0 | = | $1000$ | fixed independently of the law of units |
| q-constant-g | number of tags g | = | $10^9+1$ | fixed independently of n |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-hole-triangle-free** [claim](#stmt-lem-hole-triangle-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hole-triangle-free): Sum the functional identities around a hypothetical triangle; bilinear terms cancel by symmetry and parity gives a contradiction. -> hole relation is loopless and triangle-free
- **step-entropy-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-support): Directional derivative of relative entropy at the minimizer. -> support positivity and entropy increment
- **step-terminal-cut** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-cut): Max-flow min-cut on a capacitated network. -> small endpoint and pair exception sets
- **step-raw-to-graph** [claim](#stmt-prop-raw-to-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-raw-to-graph): Entropy-controlled fingerprint containers, union bound over terminal sets and sampling tail estimates. -> sampled graph with small connected matching number
- **step-matching-minor** [claim](#stmt-prop-matching-minor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-matching-minor): Count branch sets by size and form a touching matching from small branch sets. -> upper bound on Hadwiger number
- **step-frame-images** [claim](#stmt-lem-frame-images) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frame-images): Orbit transitivity and Gram normalization give uniform image laws and rank-dependent caps. -> reference image probability caps
- **step-peeling** [claim](#stmt-lem-peeling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-peeling): Iteratively pin violating exact-image constraints, comparing density caps with image bounds. -> leaves with bounded pins and min-entropy bounds
- **step-gradient-realization** [claim](#stmt-thm-gradient-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-gradient-realization): Solve a relaxed linear problem, control the correction rank by compression, factor through channels. -> cross Gram entries giving four holes
- **step-collision** [claim](#stmt-prop-collision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-collision): Prune small record cells, bound image entropy and test scalar Gram entries by Walsh estimates. -> four-hole probability lower bound
- **step-phase-alternative** [claim](#stmt-thm-phase-alternative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-cover-rank-2): High-moment cancellation without a cover; offset records and Walsh estimates with a cover. -> phase dichotomy
- **step-status-preparation** [claim](#stmt-prop-status-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-zero-delta-2): Case analysis over predictions, injection and phase alternatives. -> prepared unit law in one of three regimes
- **step-weak-product** [claim](#stmt-thm-weak-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-terminal-remainder-2): Twisted regularity recursion with terminal remainders controlled by four-copy estimates. -> unary product approximation
- **step-positive-overlap** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-overlap): Assume vanishing overlap, build a common limit, contradict via fresh generator lists. -> positive query overlap
- **step-rare-overlap** [claim](#stmt-prop-rare-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rare-overlap): Superlevel nets on one leaf plus uniform large-set hit estimate on the independent opposite leaf. -> inverse-polynomial overlap
- **step-main-completion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rare-overlap-3): Argue by contradiction along a violating sequence, then transfer to the graph. -> graphs with independence number at most two and small connected matching

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-hadwiger-matching-bound**: For every finite nonempty graph of order m, the Hadwiger number is at most (m plus four times the connected matching number plus two) divided by three; a finite check on small graphs compares the exactly computed Hadwiger number and connected matching number against this bound.
- **inv-small-matching-chromatic-gap**: When the independence number is at most two and the connected matching number is below one hundredth of the order, the Hadwiger number is strictly below half the order, which is at most the chromatic number (order at least five).

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-hadwiger-upper-bound-on-chromatic**: Use the clique-minor number of a graph as a guaranteed upper bound on its chromatic number or fractional chromatic number. -> The source constructs graphs where the chromatic number and even the fractional chromatic number exceed the Hadwiger number, so the bound fails.
- **anti-coarse-bound-as-exact**: Report the counting bound as the value or a tight estimate of the Hadwiger number. -> The bound is one-sided and can be far from the true value, as on paths and sparse graphs.
- **anti-bruteforce-on-construction**: Run the exhaustive reference procedure on the graphs produced by the paper's construction to confirm the connected-matching bound. -> The sampled graph has doubly exponential order in the construction parameter, so exhaustive enumeration is infeasible and the harness proves nothing at that scale.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The counting inequality relating the Hadwiger number to the connected matching number is checkable by a finite procedure on small graphs: compute the connected matching number and the Hadwiger number exactly and compare with the bound. The paper's main existence theorem is not checked here.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens: m e followed by e pairs of vertex indices (0-based), simple graph, m between 1 and 6
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= m <= 6, no repeated edges, indices in range
# OUTPUTS: one line 'cm h' with the connected matching number and the Hadwiger number
# TIME: exponential in m (enumerates edge subsets and vertex labelings), fine for m <= 6
# SPACE: O(m^2) adjacency matrix plus the current labeling
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer arithmetic only
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys
import itertools

def main():
    t = sys.stdin.read().split()
    m = int(t[0])
    e = int(t[1])
    adj = [[False] * m for _ in range(m)]
    edges = set()
    for i in range(e):
        u = int(t[2 + 2 * i])
        v = int(t[3 + 2 * i])
        if u != v:
            edges.add((min(u, v), max(u, v)))
            adj[u][v] = True
            adj[v][u] = True
    el = sorted(edges)
    cm = 0
    for r in range(1, len(el) + 1):
        found = False
        for S in itertools.combinations(el, r):
            ok = True
            for a in range(r):
                for b in range(a + 1, r):
                    x = S[a]
                    y = S[b]
                    if set(x) & set(y):
                        ok = False
                        break
                    if not any(adj[p][q] for p in x for q in y):
                        ok = False
                        break
                if not ok:
                    break
            if ok:
                found = True
                break
        if found:
            cm = r
        else:
            break
    def connected(vs):
        seen = {vs[0]}
        st = [vs[0]]
        while st:
            x = st.pop()
            for y in vs:
                if y not in seen and adj[x][y]:
                    seen.add(y)
                    st.append(y)
        return len(seen) == len(vs)
    h = 0
    for k in range(m, 0, -1):
        for lab in itertools.product(range(k + 1), repeat=m):
            groups = [[v for v in range(m) if lab[v] == c] for c in range(1, k + 1)]
            if any(len(g) == 0 for g in groups):
                continue
            if not all(connected(g) for g in groups):
                continue
            good = True
            for a in range(k):
                for b in range(a + 1, k):
                    if not any(adj[p][q] for p in groups[a] for q in groups[b]):
                        good = False
                        break
                if not good:
                    break
            if good:
                h = k
                break
        if h:
            break
    print(cm, h)

main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result only asserts that graphs of arbitrarily large order exist, via a probabilistic sampling argument. No procedure that outputs a graph or a verified minor bound is stated, so there is nothing to implement as a library or service. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sampled graphs live on an astronomically large vertex set determined by an exponential parameter, so no simulation or procedural-content use can instantiate them. The paper offers no gameplay or physical-simulation structure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker, solver or verifier is specified. The proposition bounding the Hadwiger number by connected matchings gives an inequality, but computing the connected matching number is not part of the paper. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is an existence proof by container counting and supersaturation. Its deterministic steps (fingerprint replay, residual cut) are proof devices over exponentially large spaces, not an algorithm with usable input, output or cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper defines algebraic objects (cut profiles, moment spaces, frames) over a binary field for a proof. They are never proposed as storage or indexing structures with operations and costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel or dataflow is given, and the object sizes grow exponentially in the dimension parameter. The binary-field linear algebra is used only inside proofs. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no security definition, hardness assumption or adversary. The triangle-free hole relation is a combinatorial device with no cryptographic reading. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Entropy minimization and Pinsker-type bounds appear only as proof tools for finite probability laws on raw units. No model, data set, loss or generalization claim is made. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Fractional chromatic number is discussed only to extend the lower bound to the fractional relaxation. The result is a gap between fractional chromatic number and Hadwiger number, not a solver, a formulation to optimise, or an approximation guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no protocol, message model, fault model or consensus notion. Independence between sampled units is a probabilistic device, not a distributed computation. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only transferable content is mathematical: coefficient-one Hadwiger-type bounds must not be assumed in any downstream reasoning about graphs with independence number at most two. This is a warning about use, not an engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-independence-two-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The gap between Hadwiger number and chromatic number is established only for graphs whose independence number is at most two and whose connected matching number is below a hundredth of the order. No gap is claimed for graphs outside this class. Blocked use: ['Asserting that an arbitrary graph has chromatic number above its Hadwiger number', 'Applying the order-over-two chromatic lower bound to graphs with larger independence number']
- **limit-small-order-edge** (edge_case): The strict chain from the Hadwiger bound to half the order is derived only when the order satisfies a lower threshold, so very small graphs are not covered by the displayed inequality. Blocked use: ['Using the strict inequality between the Hadwiger bound and half the order on graphs of order below five', 'Quoting the corollary chain as valid for every order']
- **limit-coarse-minor-bound** (approximation): The counting estimate on the Hadwiger number is deliberately coarse. It is a one-sided ceiling and can be far from the true value on many graphs. Blocked use: ['Reporting the right-hand side of the counting bound as the Hadwiger number', 'Using the bound as a tight estimate in an algorithm that needs the exact clique-minor size']
- **limit-nonconstructive-existence** (nonconstructive): The main theorem and raw supersaturation are obtained by a probabilistic sampling argument, and the supersaturation statement is proved by contradiction along subsequences. No explicit graph, algorithm or constants-free procedure is produced. Blocked use: ['Presenting the theorem as a graph generator with a runnable output', 'Extracting explicit onset values of the dimension parameter from the existence proof', 'Claiming the sampled graph is certified deterministically rather than with high probability']
- **limit-parameter-scale** (formal_scope): The sampled graph order is exponential in the ambient dimension, and the construction parameters are enormous constants fixed before the asymptotic parameter grows. The statement is purely asymptotic. Blocked use: ['Exhaustive or simulated verification of the connected-matching bound on the constructed graphs', 'Using the construction to produce graphs of a moderate prescribed order']
- **limit-binary-field-model** (formal_scope): All algebraic objects (frames, moments, forms, ranks) live over the two-element field. The hole relation and its triangle-freeness depend on this setting and on the symmetric form chosen. Blocked use: ['Transplanting the hole construction to another coefficient field without re-proving triangle-freeness', 'Reading the cut-profile space as a data structure with stated operations']
- **limit-weakening-coloring-side** (counterexample_scope): The fractional relaxation is on the coloring side only; the clique-minor side remains the ordinary Hadwiger number. The counterexample does not refute statements with other minor invariants or a fixed constant above one. Blocked use: ['Concluding that no linear relaxation with a constant coefficient holds', 'Applying the refutation to a fractional version of the minor number']

>
> - [A linear list-coloring bound in terms of the Hadwiger number](../../preprints/A-linear-list-coloring-bound-in-terms-of-the-Hadwiger-number-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [A counterexample to Hadwiger's conjecture](../../preprints/A-counterexample-to-Hadwigers-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Hadwiger's conjecture predicts $\chi(G)\le h(G)$ for every finite graph, where $h(G)$ is the largest clique-minor order. The formalization constructs arbitrarily large finite simple counterexamples with independence number at most two. For a graph on $m$ vertices it proves
> $h(G)<26m/75+2/3<m/2\le\chi(G)$.
> This disproves the ordinary chromatic form. The paper's fractional-chromatic strengthening and the Colin de Verdière consequence are outside this selected statement.
>
> The Linear List Hadwiger conjecture asks for a universal linear bound on list chromatic number in terms of clique-minor size. The formalization proves that there is one integer $C\ge1$ such that every finite nonempty simple graph $G$ satisfies $\chi_{\mathrm{list}}(G)\le C h(G)$, where $h(G)$ is the largest order of a clique minor. The constant is independent of the graph.

Comparator source `HadwigerCounterexample.lean` (1765 bytes, sha256 `83f9f9c49f49d4abc35c8d1b7247ccab5de9c9ef909c45acabc16875e306b898`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace HadwigerMatching

universe u
variable {V : Type u}

def IsTouchingMatching (G : SimpleGraph V) (M : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
  (∀ e ∈ M, e.card = 2 ∧ G.IsClique (e : Set V)) ∧
  (∀ e ∈ M, ∀ f ∈ M, e ≠ f → Disjoint e f) ∧
  (∀ e ∈ M, ∀ f ∈ M, e ≠ f →
    ∃ v ∈ e, ∃ w ∈ f, G.Adj v w)

noncomputable def connectedMatchingNumber (G : SimpleGraph V) : ℕ :=
  sSup {n : ℕ | ∃ M : Finset (Finset V), IsTouchingMatching G M ∧ M.card = n}

end HadwigerMatching

namespace LinearListHadwiger

def HasCliqueMinor {V : Type} (G : SimpleGraph V) (t : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ B : Fin t → Set V,
    (∀ i, (G.induce (B i)).Connected) ∧
    (∀ i j, i ≠ j → Disjoint (B i) (B j)) ∧
    (∀ i j, i ≠ j → ∃ v ∈ B i, ∃ w ∈ B j, G.Adj v w)

noncomputable def hadwigerNumber {V : Type} (G : SimpleGraph V) : ℕ :=
  sSup {t : ℕ | HasCliqueMinor G t}

end LinearListHadwiger

namespace HadwigerCounterexample

open HadwigerMatching LinearListHadwiger

theorem exists_counterexamples (M : ℕ) : ∃ m : ℕ, max M 5 ≤ m ∧
    ∃ G : SimpleGraph (Fin m), G.indepNum ≤ 2 ∧
      (connectedMatchingNumber G : ℚ) < (m : ℚ) / 100 ∧
      (hadwigerNumber G : ℚ) < 26 * (m : ℚ) / 75 + 2 / 3 ∧
      26 * (m : ℚ) / 75 + 2 / 3 < (m : ℚ) / 2 ∧
      (m : ℚ) / 2 ≤ (G.chromaticNumber.toNat : ℚ) ∧
      hadwigerNumber G < G.chromaticNumber.toNat ∧
      G.chromaticNumber ≠ ⊤ ∧
      G.chromaticNumber = ↑(G.chromaticNumber.toNat) := by
  sorry

theorem not_hadwiger_conjecture :
    ¬ (∀ (m : ℕ) (G : SimpleGraph (Fin m)),
      G.chromaticNumber ≤ ↑(hadwigerNumber G)) := by
  sorry

end HadwigerCounterexample

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove Hadwiger's conjecture by constructing arbitrarily large graphs whose chromatic number exceeds their Hadwiger number. The examples have independence number at most two, and even their ordinary fractional chromatic number exceeds their Hadwiger number. Thus they also disprove the fractional-coloring weakening discussed by Reed and Seymour.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a finite graph $G$, write $\alpha(G)$, $\chi(G)$, and $h(G)$ for its independence number, chromatic number, and Hadwiger number. Thus $h(G)$ is the largest $t$ for which $G$ contains a $K_t$ minor.

Hadwiger’s conjecture, formulated in 1943 (Hadwiger 1943) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[hadwiger1943](#ref-hadwiger1943)], asserts that $h(G)\ge\chi(G)$ for every finite nonempty simple graph $G$. We disprove it using graphs of independence number at most two.

Two disjoint edges are *touching* if an edge joins their endpoint sets. A *connected matching* is a matching whose edges are pairwise touching; write $\mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)$ for its maximum size. The word connected in this definition requires adjacency of every pair of matching edges.

Our main result is the following.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There are arbitrarily large integers $m$ for which an $m$-vertex finite simple graph $G$ satisfies $$\alpha(G)\le2
             \qquad\text{and}\qquad
             \mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)<\frac{m}{100}.$$*

</div>

A complete minor with many singleton or two-vertex branch sets gives a large connected matching. The precise counting inequality, proved in [prop:matching-minor](#prop:matching-minor), is $$h(G)\le\frac{|V(G)|+4\mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)+2}{3}.$$ Every color class has size at most $\alpha(G)$, so $\chi(G)\ge |V(G)|/\alpha(G)$. The same lower bound holds for fractional coloring, giving a second consequence of the construction. For a finite nonempty simple graph $G$, let $\mathcal I(G)$ be the family of its independent vertex sets. A fractional coloring assigns a nonnegative real weight $w_I$ to every $I\in\mathcal I(G)$ such that $\sum_{I\ni v}w_I\ge1$ for every vertex $v$. The ordinary fractional chromatic number $\chi_f(G)$ is the minimum of $\sum_Iw_I$ over these assignments. An ordinary coloring gives a fractional coloring with unit weights on its color classes, so $\chi_f(G)\le\chi(G)$.

<a id="stmt-cor-fractional-hadwiger"></a>
<div id="cor:fractional-hadwiger" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *<span id="cor:hadwiger" label="cor:hadwiger"></span>There are finite nonempty simple graphs $G$ of arbitrarily large order $m$ for which $$h(G)<\frac{26m}{75}+\frac23
 <\frac m2\le\chi_f(G)\le\chi(G).$$ Thus Hadwiger’s conjecture and its fractional-coloring weakening $\chi_f(G)\le h(G)$ are false.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-fractional-hadwiger"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-fractional-hadwiger</summary>

*Proof.* Summing the vertex constraints of any fractional coloring gives $$|V(G)|\le\sum_{I\in\mathcal I(G)}|I|w_I
       \le\alpha(G)\sum_{I\in\mathcal I(G)}w_I.$$ Hence $\chi_f(G)\ge |V(G)|/\alpha(G)$. Apply [thm:main](#thm:main) and the minor bound above, noting that $26m/75+2/3<m/2$ for $m\ge5$. ◻

</details>

Reed and Seymour (1998, 148) discussed the weakening that, for every positive integer $p$, a graph with no $K_{p+1}$ minor should have a fractional $p$-coloring, and proved the corresponding $2p$-bound in their result (1.3). Their convention uses nonnegative rational weights, coverage exactly one at each vertex, and total weight at most $p$, so every such coloring is admissible above. Taking $p=h(G)$ in the corollary therefore disproves this prediction as well. The relaxation is on the coloring side; $h(G)$ remains the ordinary clique-minor number.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical context

Hadwiger’s conjecture places planar coloring in a broader minor-theoretic setting. The $K_4$-minor-free case was proved independently by Hadwiger (Hadwiger 1943) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[hadwiger1943](#ref-hadwiger1943)] and Dirac (Dirac 1952) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Dirac1952](#ref-Dirac1952)]. Wagner’s structure theorem (Wagner 1937) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Wagner1937](#ref-Wagner1937)] makes the four-colorability of graphs without a $K_5$ minor equivalent to the Four-Color Theorem, proved by Appel and Haken, with Koch contributing to the reducibility part (Appel and Haken 1977; Appel, Haken, and Koch 1977) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[AppelHaken1977](#ref-AppelHaken1977), [AppelHakenKoch1977](#ref-AppelHakenKoch1977)]. Robertson, Seymour, and Thomas (Robertson, Seymour, and Thomas 1993) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[RobertsonSeymourThomas1993](#ref-RobertsonSeymourThomas1993)] proved that every graph without a $K_6$ minor is five-colorable, by reducing the obstruction to a graph that becomes planar after one vertex is removed. These results explain the conjecture’s link between coloring and the structure of minor-closed classes.

For general $t$, the density bounds of Kostochka (Kostochka 1984) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Kostochka1984](#ref-Kostochka1984)] and Thomason (Thomason 1984) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Thomason1984](#ref-Thomason1984)] give $O(t\sqrt{\log t})$-colorability for graphs with no $K_t$ minor. Norin, Postle, and Song (Norin, Postle, and Song 2023, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[NorinPostleSong2023](#ref-NorinPostleSong2023)] broke this degeneracy barrier, obtaining $O(t(\log t)^\beta)$ colors for every fixed $\beta>1/4$. Delcourt and Postle (Delcourt and Postle 2025, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DelcourtPostle2025](#ref-DelcourtPostle2025)] reduced the bound to $O(t\log\log t)$. A recent preprint of Liu and Luo (Liu and Luo 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[LiuLuo2026](#ref-LiuLuo2026)] further gives $O(t\log\log\log t)$-colorability.

The restriction of Hadwiger’s conjecture to graphs of independence number at most two has an equivalent formulation: every such graph on $m$ vertices should have a $K_{\lceil m/2\rceil}$ minor. Plummer, Stiebitz, and Toft (Plummer, Stiebitz, and Toft 2003, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[pst2003](#ref-pst2003)] proved equivalence between the restricted conjecture and this universally quantified minor assertion. This identifies the case $\alpha(G)\le2$ as a setting in which chromatic number can be replaced by an explicit fraction of the order.

Duchet and Meyniel (Duchet and Meyniel 1982) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[duchet1982](#ref-duchet1982)] proved the general lower bound $h(G)\ge |V(G)|/(2\alpha(G)-1)$, giving $h(G)\ge m/3$ when $\alpha(G)=2$. Blasiak (Blasiak 2007) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Blasiak2007](#ref-Blasiak2007)] obtained complete-minor models of order $\Omega(m^{4/5})$ using only singleton and edge branch sets. Fox (Fox 2010, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[fox2010](#ref-fox2010)] strengthened the $m/3$ bound to $$h(G)\ge \frac m3+c\,m^{4/5}(\log m)^{1/5}$$ for an absolute constant $c>0$. His Theorem 3 also yields a connected matching of size $\Omega(m^{4/5}(\log m)^{1/5})$, since its alternative clique of the same order can be paired. These bounds describe the quantitative background to the fixed linear upper bounds in [thm:main](#thm:main), [cor:hadwiger](#cor:hadwiger).

Füredi, Gyárfás, and Simonyi (Füredi, Gyárfás, and Simonyi 2005) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[fgs2005](#ref-fgs2005)] proposed the sharp connected-matching conjecture: a graph with independence number two and order $4t-1$ has a connected matching of size $t$. They verified the assertion through $t=17$, and Chen and Deng (Chen and Deng 2025) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ChenDeng2025](#ref-ChenDeng2025)] extended this range to $t=22$. Cambie (Cambie 2021, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[cambie2021](#ref-cambie2021)] proved that Hadwiger’s conjecture implies this statement. The graphs in [thm:main](#thm:main) also disprove the sharp connected-matching conjecture: delete at most three vertices to obtain order $4t-1$; the connected-matching number does not increase and remains below $t$ for sufficiently large orders. The remaining graph has independence number exactly two, since a complete graph of order $4t-1$ has a connected matching of size $2t-1$.

Substantial positive results also come from additional clique structure. Chudnovsky and Seymour (Chudnovsky and Seymour 2012, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[ChudnovskySeymour2012](#ref-ChudnovskySeymour2012)] proved that an $m$-vertex graph with independence number at most two has a $K_{\lceil m/2\rceil}$ minor whenever its clique number is at least $m/4$ for even $m$, or at least $(m+3)/4$ for odd $m$. Their method packs induced three-vertex paths. Norin and Seymour (Norin and Seymour 2026, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[NorinSeymour2026](#ref-NorinSeymour2026)] subsequently combined this structure with random pairing to obtain minors of order $\chi(G)$ and edge density at least $\gamma-o(1)$, where $\gamma=0.986882\ldots$ is a fixed constant. These results highlight the requirement in a complete-minor model: every pair of branch sets must be joined, whereas high density permits missing adjacencies.

A weaker conjecture asks only for some absolute $c_0>0$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)\ge c_0|V(G)|$ for all sufficiently large graphs with $\alpha(G)\le2$ (Füredi, Gyárfás, and Simonyi 2005) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[fgs2005](#ref-fgs2005)]. Yip (Yip 2025, Theorem 7) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[yip2025](#ref-yip2025)] proved a dense variant: a matching of linear size can have the proportion of its non-touching edge pairs made arbitrarily small, with the linear coefficient depending on the prescribed proportion. That statement allows such pairs to remain. The fixed coefficient in [thm:main](#thm:main) leaves the weaker conjecture for smaller positive coefficients unresolved; it does not assert $\mathop{\mathrm{cm}}(G)=o(|V(G)|)$.

A related parity-constrained problem has a different recent resolution. An odd $K_t$ minor consists of $t$ disjoint trees, each properly two-colored, with a same-color edge joining every pair of trees. Kühn, Sauermann, Steiner, and Wigderson (Kühn et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KuhnSauermannSteinerWigderson2025](#ref-KuhnSauermannSteinerWigderson2025)] disproved the odd Hadwiger conjecture using graphs of independence number at most two. The construction in this paper bounds the order of ordinary complete minors.

The separate companion manuscript (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[OpenAIColinDeVerdiereCounterexample2026](#ref-OpenAIColinDeVerdiereCounterexample2026)] constructs graphs with $\chi(G)>\mu(G)+1$, where $\mu$ is the ordinary Colin de Verdière invariant. Since $h(G)\le\mu(G)+1$, its result also gives counterexamples to Hadwiger’s inequality. The argument here proves the connected-matching bound directly and does not use that invariant.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The construction and its proof

We build $G$ by sampling from a finite probability space and declaring some pairs to be *holes*, or missing edges. The hole relation has no loops or triangles. Its complement on the sampled positions therefore has independence number at most two, even when sampled elements repeat. An ordered pair with no internal hole is called a *unit*; it represents a possible matching edge. Two units conflict when all four cross pairs are holes, as in [fig:four-holes](#fig:four-holes). Such edges cannot belong to one connected matching.

The main distribution theorem forces conflicts between independent units drawn from any law with specified marginal and joint density bounds. It permits arbitrary dependence between the endpoints of a unit, as is necessary when the units come from a matching. An entropy-minimizing container argument transfers this statement to the finite sample: while a family of units supports such a law, supersaturation supplies a conflict neighborhood to delete; when no such law remains, a capacitated cut covers the residual family by a small set of endpoint types and a small set of pairs.

<figure id="fig:four-holes">

<figcaption>All four cross holes make the two solid matching edges non-touching. The distribution theorem forces this conflict in every unit law satisfying its marginal and joint density bounds.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-four-holes"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:four-holes`)

```text
Four circular vertices arranged in two vertical pairs representing two units. On the left, labeled "unit A", vertex $A_1$ (top) is connected to vertex $A_2$ (bottom) by a vertical solid line. On the right, labeled "unit B", vertex $B_1$ (top) is connected to vertex $B_2$ (bottom) by a vertical solid line. Four dashed red lines connect the pairs across the two units: horizontal dashed lines connecting $A_1$ to $B_1$ and $A_2$ to $B_2$, and diagonal dashed lines connecting $A_1$ to $B_2$ and $A_2$ to $B_1$. Below the diagram is a legend indicating: "solid: edges of G" and "dashed: holes".
```

To define holes, each sampled configuration carries injective linear maps into common binary vector spaces. The coefficient spaces record monomial evaluations $v$ at Boolean points; their point moments are the rank-one matrices $vv^{\mathsf T}$. The frame maps induce an embedding $U_o$ of a space built from these moments, together with a linear functional $u_o$ on the ambient tensor space. Auxiliary frame columns will be used to impose the cross-contraction equations. A hole is certified by a shared tensor and two prescribed identities for the cross contractions. The identities come from a symmetric bilinear form; summing them around a hypothetical triangle cancels every bilinear term and leaves $0=1$. [sec:geometry](#sec:geometry) proves this parity mechanism before constructing the particular moments and frames.

Forcing four holes is the harder task. The construction has one unbounded parameter $n$, and the ambient vector dimension is $N=M_0n$, with $M_0$ fixed before the limit. The three main steps are as follows.

1.  *Finite certificates for four holes ().* The candidate witnesses are short sums of point moments. A point’s *key* is the tuple of its images under the relevant frame maps; equal keys on the two sides give a shared tensor. We retain specified images of a bounded number of coefficient directions, called *pins*. A fixed Boolean selector labels the points; interpolation and a bounded set of excluded labels make the key directions independent modulo these pins. A separate low-rank moment decomposition identifies the obstructions to solving the cross-contraction equations. Once the corresponding scalar conditions hold, the remaining equations cost only $O(n)$ bits. The collision criterion in [prop:collision](#prop:collision) reduces four holes to overlap of the accepted key distributions. The witness sums use paired lists of points, and a *scalar recipe* prescribes their scalar conditions. Sampling the point parameters with the configuration fixed will be called a *query*; its full key is the list of the point keys.

2.  *Preparation of the unit law ().* Conditioning on exact pin images decomposes the law into parts called *leaves*, with control of every further independent image prescription. A *small table* records the cross pairings of the projected pin directions and the auxiliary frame directions. The scalar conditions must be compatible with this table. Phase estimates produce compatible tables on a positive mass of independent unit pairs, or on a smaller mass at least a fixed multiple of $N^{-4}$ determined by their leaves.

3.  *Forcing overlap ().* Each leaf’s accepted key distributions have finite $L^1$ approximating families. Estimates for the mixed law also control tests of single keys and the scalar values recorded with them. A product comparison connects these single-position estimates to tests of the full key tuple. If overlap vanished in the positive-mass case, a common limit would rule out every scalar recipe whose matched positions have positive overlap; finite-dimensional duality produces precisely such a recipe. The inverse-polynomial compatibility mass may disappear in this limit, so it is handled separately: a uniform large-set estimate is applied to a finite family chosen on one leaf before the independent opposite leaf is sampled.

The Boolean moment argument uses commuting multiplication operators, as in the flat-extension approach to moment problems (Laurent and Mourrain 2009) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[laurentmourrain2009](#ref-laurentmourrain2009)]. The distribution-to-graph argument combines information projection (Csiszár 1975) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[csiszar1975](#ref-csiszar1975)], independent-set fingerprints (Kleitman and Winston 1982; Samotij 2015) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[kleitmanwinston1982](#ref-kleitmanwinston1982), [samotij2015](#ref-samotij2015)], and a capacitated cut (Ford and Fulkerson 1956) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[fordfulkerson1956](#ref-fordfulkerson1956)]. The overlap estimates use entropy (Cover and Thomas 2006) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[cover2006](#ref-cover2006)] and the energy-increment method of weak regularity (Frieze and Kannan 1999) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[frieze1999](#ref-frieze1999)]; the additional constraints from the frame pairings are retained explicitly in their proofs.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Organization and conventions

define the frames and holes and reduce the graph problem to uniform supersaturation. [sec:frame-laws](#sec:frame-laws) establishes the frame-image estimates and the decomposition into pinned leaves. [sec:moments](#sec:moments), [sec:realization](#sec:realization), [sec:collision](#sec:collision) prove the algebraic realization and collision criteria. [sec:phases](#sec:phases), [sec:preparation](#sec:preparation) prepare the unit law and its small tables. The analytic comparison tools are established in [sec:histograms](#sec:histograms), [sec:product-tests](#sec:product-tests). [sec:common-limit](#sec:common-limit), [sec:status-span](#sec:status-span) prove overlap on the branches with positive limiting compatibility mass, and [sec:rare-overlap](#sec:rare-overlap) handles the remaining branch and completes the proof. [sec:parameters](#sec:parameters) collects the order of choices and the quantitative margins.

All vector spaces, matrices, ranks, and algebraic forms are over $\mathbb F_2$. Probabilities, densities, norms, and entropies are real. Logarithms in bit counts have base two; changing the base for entropy only changes fixed constants. Constants described as bounded may be very large but are independent of $n$. An $O(n)$ scalar record has its coefficient fixed before $M_0$ is chosen. Later fixed test accuracies and partition sizes may change how large $n$ must be; they do not change the construction.

The two endpoints in one unit may be dependent. Separate units, and their leaves before additional pairing conditions, are sampled independently. Query subdensities retain the mass lost to all acceptance tests.

<a id="sec:geometry"></a>
### The hole relation and its tensor realization

The construction has two tasks. Its missing-edge relation must be triangle-free, so that the resulting graph has independence number at most two. It must also provide enough missing edges to prevent a large connected matching. We first give the algebraic reason for triangle-freeness. The tensor and frame construction that follows provides the additional structure needed for the second task.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### A triangle-free relation

Let $\mathcal X$ and $\mathcal V$ be finite-dimensional vector spaces over $\mathbb F_2$. Fix a linear functional $a\in\mathcal X^*$ and a symmetric bilinear form $T$ on $\mathcal X$, and write $$r(x)=a+T(x,\cdot)\in\mathcal X^*.$$ Let $\Omega$ be a finite set. For each $i\in\Omega$, suppose we have an injective linear map $U_i:\mathcal X\to\mathcal V$ and a linear functional $u_i\in\mathcal V^*$.

<a id="stmt-def-hole"></a>
<div id="def:hole" class="definition">

**Definition 3**. Two elements $i,j\in\Omega$ have a *hole* between them if there exist $\lambda_i,\lambda_j\in\mathcal X$ such that <a id="eq:hole-equations"></a>$$\label{eq:hole-equations}
 \begin{gathered}
 U_i\lambda_i=U_j\lambda_j,\qquad
 u_jU_i=r(\lambda_i),\qquad u_iU_j=r(\lambda_j),\\
 a(\lambda_i)+a(\lambda_j)=1.
 \end{gathered}$$ The middle two identities are identities of linear functionals on the whole space $\mathcal X$.

</div>

Thus a hole has a shared vector in $\mathcal V$, one witness for it in each copy of $\mathcal X$, and opposite values of $a$ on the witnesses. The functional identities make these requirements incompatible around a triangle.

<a id="stmt-lem-hole-triangle-free"></a>
<div id="lem:hole-triangle-free" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *The hole relation is symmetric, has no loops, and is triangle-free.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-hole-triangle-free"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-hole-triangle-free</summary>

*Proof.* Symmetry follows by interchanging the endpoints. A loop would have $U_i\lambda_i=U_i\lambda_j$, hence $\lambda_i=\lambda_j$ by injectivity, contradicting the last equation of [eq:hole-equations](#eq:hole-equations).

Suppose that three distinct elements have holes on all three pairs. For a directed incidence $ij$, let $x_{ij}$ be its witness at $i$. For distinct $i,j,k$, evaluate the functional identity for $ij$ on $x_{ik}$: $$u_j(U_ix_{ik})=a(x_{ik})+T(x_{ij},x_{ik}).$$ Sum over the six ordered choices of $(i,j,k)$. For fixed $j$, the two arguments on the left are equal by the sharing equation for the remaining edge $ik$, so they cancel. For fixed $i$, the two bilinear terms cancel by symmetry of $T$. The remaining sum is $$\sum_{\{i,j\}}\bigl(a(x_{ij})+a(x_{ji})\bigr)=1+1+1=1,$$ a contradiction. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:sample-independence"></a>
Given any list $o_1,\ldots,o_m\in\Omega$, form a graph $G$ on its *positions*: two distinct positions are adjacent when their elements have no hole. This gives a finite simple graph even when elements repeat. Equal elements are adjacent because holes have no loops. An independent triple of positions would therefore give three distinct elements forming a hole triangle. Hence $$\label{eq:sample-independence}
 \alpha(G)\le2,\qquad \chi(G)\ge\lceil m/2\rceil.$$
